## November 21, 2026 — creation readiness, chat connection and failed-build recovery repair

The owner reported that the coach did not reliably recognize a buildable idea, chat appeared offline and unnatural, and the failed-build retry led to a blocking connection dialog. The repair is based on merged main `c03287a885014812d145dd8683247bdd172597cb` (PR112), preserving the earlier guided-creation and game-quality work. This is source implementation and fixture verification; it is not a production rollout or successful live conversation receipt.

The source retains the original authored premise across longer conversations, targets a playable first version without optional-detail interrogation, ends questioning once ready, and exposes visible disabled Build with explicit **Check my idea** / **Reconnect chat**. New-idea technical failure now reports a saved idea instead of running a scripted local interview. Opted-in replies missing readiness fail as technical checks; incomplete output is distinct from an actual refusal. A private status check also detects an impossible fixed prompt/output reservation without making an inference request or changing approved budget values. Actual conversation outcomes and health availability remain separate in both Studio and the adult banner.

Retry now recovers the authoritative saved job after reopening and submits the exact accepted request through the existing retry endpoint. The gateway atomically permits one same-owner successor per failed job and exposes its optional `retryJobId` on an authorized status read. A lost retry acknowledgement recovers that successor through GETs; a failed successor still needs another explicit action. The handoff exposes retry for known failure and status recovery for known-job uncertainty. A new initial POST without an acknowledged job ID remains a distinct limitation: this repair does not add durable pending-request storage or request-ID lookup, and an older failed job must not clear its uncertainty.

Focused executable tests cover authored history, minimum-playable evidence, ready-question suppression, explicit reassessment without a fake child message, legacy transcript recovery, refusal/technical distinction, provider preflight, concurrent retry admission, lost retry acknowledgement, exact request reuse and adult isolation. Browser fixtures additionally cover visible Build/reconnect, reopened failed jobs, actionable handoffs, owner changes and health-versus-actual-chat outcomes. The local production build/typecheck and test receipts are recorded in the associated PR; local Chromium cannot start in this execution environment (`socket() failed: Operation not permitted`), so local browser attempts are blocked infrastructure evidence rather than passes. Hosted browser and service packaging checks must run on the published combined source.

The public Pages marker observed during this repair still reported PR108 source `c43a18148421ea22edfba5f7e891530678ad4f4f`, and public gateway health reported ready/paid execution enabled. PR113's operator receipt reports the later PR112 gateway repair at 100% traffic; that receipt is owner-provided and was not independently confirmed with authenticated Cloud configuration access here. Scoped diagnostic log access was unavailable, and the available analytics project had no recent application telemetry to identify the specific live chat failure. Health and synthetic responses do not establish provider connectivity or natural conversational quality.

Release needs compatible gateway, private coach and browser source, with the additive retry recovery contract present before the browser depends on it. Preserve serving configuration, approvals, model/token/budget values, adult authorization and existing generation-worker/sandbox pins. No worker executable, paid activation, credential, permission or live setting is changed by this repair. A bounded authorized real conversation and Build → recovery/Review → Play → Keep journey remain required; issue93 is not closed by these fixtures. Do not replay the historical PR109 fixed-source backend release over the newer gateway repair.

## November 21, 2026 — conversation-led creation corrected and locally verified

The owner rejected the provisional answer-chip/delegation interpretation and clarified that the conversation coach must guide children to their own answers, with no new game until enough child-authored intent exists to make a reasonable game. [The decision record](docs/decisions/2026-11-20-guided-chat-creation.md) preserves that correction. This candidate is on `feat/guided-creation-20261120`, based on main **`d0d05944ea4957aef51b690bf02dc8f6bb73f04e`**. The published PR records its resulting source SHA; this entry does not identify a merge or deployment.

The ordinary creation workspace is the contextual coach, one composer, Speak and Send. Build appears only after a current model readiness assessment grounded in exact child-authored evidence. The host checks provenance and whether the reviewed conversation is still current; it does not pretend that quote validation proves semantic coherence. The model must identify and resolve material gaps for the particular game. Supplied-answer chips, delegation shortcuts, helper proposals, legacy plan approval, offline fallback and the optional plan cannot authorize first creation. A changed or unsent thought, pending reply or failed plan save blocks Build until resolved.

An explicit Build immediately opens a focused, truthful handoff. Play while I wait becomes available after durable acknowledgement; a ready result still requires explicit play. Background polling does not reopen the dialog. Compiled requests and lifecycle logs stay outside the child's transcript. Extra tools, plan details and the new-idea art entrance are under More choices. Decorative reward popups stay out of active chat while earned receipts, ranks and gems remain intact. Drafts, profile isolation, learning boundaries and explicit Keep retain their existing authority.

**Final local production build:** `index-qbE__9jW.js` / `index-CFCtGqFy.css`, with typecheck passed. [Four inspected desktop/phone captures](docs/child-journey/previews/README.md) record exact asset SHA-256 hashes and fixture provenance.

| Corrected-source acceptance | Observed result and limits |
| --- | --- |
| Core contracts | All 376 baseline unit tests passed. Nine guided-compilation, 14 creation-readiness and three conversation-display contracts passed, including coherent short briefs, material gaps despite filled evidence, unsupported/helper/legacy evidence, stale snapshots, current drafts, corrections and old-client response compatibility. |
| Coach integration | Full `npm run test:coach` passed, including 67 coach units, narrator, HTTP/private coach/evaluation boundaries, speech/voice and desktop/phone coach/experience browser suites. Connected replies in these checks are controlled fixtures, not a live model evaluation. |
| Child-authored new creation | Corrected desktop and 390px phone guided journeys passed through actual Send, contextual questions, incomplete and ready assessments, explicit single POST, unsent-thought blocking, truthful handoff, clean transcript and noninterrupting completion. All four request/draft regressions passed, including native/remote proposal continuity, profile return and a sent correction requiring a fresh review. |
| Existing journeys and presentation | Corrected-bundle Studio desktop/phone/remote, all first-creation groups, full Gem Journey, homepage, brand desktop/phone/legacy, delight and all 38 brand-review captures passed. Existing Keep/recovery, owner isolation and quiet chat rewards remain exercised. Four selected actual-app captures were visually inspected. |
| Handoff and art entrances | Current-source isolated handoff checks passed at 1280×900, 390×844 and 320×568, including truthful phases, focus, ownership, learning and dismissal. Integrated final-bundle handoff passed in the guided/Studio/first-creation journeys. Final-bundle art-entry readiness passed delayed/rejected configuration, upload/drawing focus, expanded chat entrance, owner boundaries, saved remote work and read-only retry with no paid replay. |
| Private coach packaging | Bundled and syntax-checked the executor from exactly the Dockerfile's copied source set after adding the readiness/display validation dependencies. No container, hosted service or provider was started. |

**Failures and scope retained:** the first final Gem Journey run still assumed Chat options was always visible; it was corrected to open More choices, then the full suite passed. Earlier full Studio and other pre-correction receipts are not substituted for the corrected-bundle results above. Automated and visual checks use synthetic profiles, deterministic coach/job responses and emulated viewports; live-model conversational quality, real devices and observed child use remain separate acceptance.

**Disposition:** corrected source implemented and locally verified for review. Frontend and private coach policy/schema need a coordinated rollout so the new browser can receive readiness assessments. Missing assessments fail closed for new creation. The change adds no endpoint, provider, automatic inference call or generation authorization, and does not activate or deploy anything. The production sandbox, adult activation and fixed Pages-release source recorded below retain their independent scope and pins.

## Production gateway secret-mount correction — November 20, 2026

The owner-supplied read-only Cloud Run diagnostic confirmed two historical Secret Manager volume objects for `factory-gateway-private-config`: `factory-gateway-private-config-ped-cut` at version 2 is **unmounted**, while `factory-gateway-private-config-mis-yek` at version 3 is the only volume mounted in the active container under `/var/run/factory-config`. ## First creation and contextual journey — implementation for review

The owner's November 19, 2026 direction authorized a simple first creation entry and small optional journey explanations. Branch `feature/jems-first-creation-journey` originally started from main **`df63bc2c7644a26a84a7c7e626b682e7c76c493d`**, preserving the merged navigation repairs and Gem Pal Studio method. The [accepted decision](docs/decisions/2026-11-19-first-creation-journey.md), [feature contract](docs/child-journey/FIRST-CREATION.md) and [rendered review captures](docs/child-journey/previews/README.md) describe this increment. The following observations preserve that development and verification chronology; the integration note above describes the newer base.

An eligible first visit now has one dominant **Make my first game** action and a quieter **Explore games first** choice. Optional hints follow the current task: the idea composer, actual waiting and ready states, play, a change, choosing a main version and returning. **Journey help** explains making, practice and the existing scene-video path, and can show tips again. **Done for now** saves the Studio draft before returning. The home recommendation prioritizes a saved ready result and unfinished creative work over unrelated recent play, with a direct explicit first-result action. Creation and Rewards explain scene boundaries, practice extensions and the separate meanings of gems, maker growth and skill practice.

Preferences use an optional bounded owner-keyed map in the existing notebook, with backward-compatible old-notebook reads and family/adult backup validation. They create no learning observations, reward facts or completed-creation claims. Shared global navigation retains Creation's existing autosaved-draft recovery choice. Explicit Keep, required-learning guards, source-owner checks, model/provider permissions and the reward economy remain authoritative.

These results do not claim a production generation or deployment observation.

### Narrow-header integration follow-up

The first published head's [Parent reporting workflow](https://github.com/jzwyrzyk/family-game-factory/actions/runs/44670107941) passed its build and 17 unit tests, then failed the unchanged 320-pixel overview overflow guard. Reproduction found that the new Journey help control plus the optional subject switch squeezed the profile chooser and made the document 335 pixels wide. At 390 pixels, the same conditional controls could overlap without causing document overflow.

The existing phone breakpoint now bounds the header tools to the available width and lets their flex row wrap. The rebuilt follow-up is **`index-jzesmVuQ.js` / `index-QfvbilBM.css`**. The unchanged full parent browser suite passes desktop, tablet, phone and small-phone views. Actual final header measurements show document width equal to 320/390 pixels, a 105-pixel profile chooser, separate help and subject controls, and 44–48-pixel control heights. All ten first-creation groups and four navigation groups pass again; the ordinary 390-pixel first playable card still ends at 829.75 pixels in the 844-pixel viewport. No test assertion, tolerance or evidence rule changed.

On the initial published head, documentation, trusted-device security, service packaging, all five full-visual jobs and Mind Your Jems passed. The Arcade creative job separately reported a failed physical/generated-quality step; its diagnostic and the current published-head outcomes are retained in the PR evidence rather than being treated as a successful initial run. The unchanged complete `npm run test:quality` passes locally on the header follow-up, including physical builder/driving, tablet input, generated containment and sticker journeys. That local result does not erase the earlier hosted failure or establish its cause.

### Tablet touch acceptance follow-up

The initial Arcade log identifies `tests/quality-tablet.cjs:40`: one part was present where two were expected after Start fresh, Add Bottle and Add Cardboard box touch actions. The old helper scrolled, measured a rectangle and separately tapped its coordinates. The log does not identify which input was missed or prove why. The header-follow-up head **`4119c2bc5f7159a762d15c0e9463bf894312aeed`** passed the parent, documentation, security, service, full-visual and Mind Your Jems workflows, but its Arcade physical/generated-quality step also failed; that run's diagnostic is retained in the PR.

The tablet test now uses Playwright's locator touch action, which waits for a visible, enabled, stable hit target before dispatching real touchscreen input. It adds exact post-Start-fresh and post-Bottle assertions to identify any recurrence, while retaining the final two-part/selection checks, touch capability, screenshots, layout limits, rotation, held driving and release assertions. No forced click, DOM activation, touch capability override, retry, delay or relaxed threshold was added. Independent review verified these semantics against the installed implementation. The substantive portrait and landscape journeys both pass locally against the unchanged `index-jzesmVuQ.js` / `index-QfvbilBM.css` application; current published-head CI remains the separate acceptance record.

**Disposition:** implemented for review in PR81. At the initial first-creation review, Art Studio [PR23](https://github.com/jzwyrzyk/family-game-factory/pull/23) was separate work; its subsequent merge and the current integration are recorded above and below. Gem Pal runtime/artwork, arbitrary same-game code editing and cross-device synchronization remain separate scope. This work does not merge or deploy the application, select a new release source, change service configuration or activate providers. The frozen coordinated-release source **`b6e160b9ec402d8cf7b728ab3189600628bde640`** remains unchanged.

Review found and fixed two concrete presentation defects: Creation Pause temporarily disabled its focused control, and asynchronous decoding could leave the newly revealed workshop hero blank in the keyboard/capture flow. Pause/Resume now retains focus; a hero-only synchronous decode passes the unchanged visual flow with the approved artwork visible. Recorder errors also restore the truthful video-export label. These changes preserve the underlying game, save and speech behavior.

[PR69](https://github.com/jzwyrzyk/family-game-factory/pull/69) publishes this follow-up. Initial head `bd6cb45914f51cf12e689f03e6a8449bd9a1ce1e` passed the visual workflow and documentation, but the security unit gate exposed an outdated simulated DOM in the builder contract tests. The fixture now supports SVG namespaces, text/element children and label attributes without changing the production source or removing assertions. All 11 affected contracts and the full 354-test unit suite pass locally; published-head CI is rerun after that fixture correction.

Publication, final CI and merge receipt will be recorded after they occur. The older PR57 results below do not stand in for this candidate. No production deployment, provider activation or private-worker repin is included in this task.

The following entries record earlier PR62 preparation and checks. PR62 is included in the current main above; the earlier draft/final-check wording remains scoped to those observations.

**Creation Studio integration with PR58 — November 19, 2026 (America/New_York):** published PR62 head `aa60b751eba40f2ff8ff24aacf4c164e389bff46` passed all five workflows and eleven jobs against main `8b915b26e1f4be4b1523b6803cb6549d228f3e83`. PR62 returned to draft for semantic integration. The combined branch preserves approved assets, production plans, suggested playthroughs, captured evidence and mandatory quality review alongside trusted host-held creation companions. Optional companion fallback applies to the source-generation request, retaining presentation and repair evidence; it adds no provider call. Combined implementation `0fc798d8e2131d9ffc1ee42afc1b248d3e04f489` (matching local tree `d5afceaaab0563711f91227cc56230e9190be9ee`) passes the build/typecheck, 77 creation and 354 existing unit tests, desktop/tablet authoring, eight recovery journeys, desktop/mobile child continuity and native Gem Factory desktop/tablet/phone/reduced-motion checks. The earlier green runs do not establish readiness for the changed base. No public merge, deployment, paid request or worker-pin change is performed by this preparation.

**Earlier Creation Studio merge preparation, before PR58 — November 19, 2026 (America/New_York):** [PR62](https://github.com/jzwyrzyk/family-game-factory/pull/62) started from `main` `079978f3034e007e8bbad1f43571aaa7867b5098` and now integrates verified `main` `8b915b26e1f4be4b1523b6803cb6549d228f3e83`, the PR61 coach-build verification fix. **Creation Studio development update — November 19, 2026 (America/New_York):** the owner authorized a new PR for integrated scene creation and scenario-rebuild practice. ## Historical PR55 implementation evidence and remaining release gates

The integration preserves the existing frontend and private backend boundaries. The adult switch uses the parent passphrase, operator permit, a fixed maximum 60-minute grant, HttpOnly cookie and tab-memory proof. Child records remain in their existing local namespace; adult jobs and revisions have separate audience ownership. Revocation/expiry are checked at paid admission and completion, worker execution/publication and preview access. The parent flow retains pending versus independently verified scopes, withdrawal and authenticated completed-receipt erasure.

The earlier PR55 integration record reported **27 passing local adult-workspace and parent-consent tests**, including expired/locked access, cross-profile operations and original-grant publication. TypeScript checking passed at that review. Additional HTTP/browser tests cover the profile switch, proof propagation, delayed-transaction expiry and late-result handling. Full application regression and exact PR-head CI were still in progress when that evidence was recorded. The subsequent merge observation above does not relabel those results as a complete test of the combined quality branch or silently refresh their scope.

The private coach was also bundled from only the Dockerfile's COPY inputs, with an isolated final directory containing one entrypoint and only a Node built-in runtime import. A keyless inactive local process refused model/audio/status probes and stayed running. Docker was unavailable locally: this check ran Node 24 with a Node 22 compilation target and does **not** replace the actual Node 22 image/non-root CI job.

No production site, cloud resource, provider key, paid flag, private-worker pin or live allowance was changed by this integration task. The existing worker PR13 still proposes the earlier PR43 source; before deployment it must select the final approved application source with the corresponding immutable image/runtime and trust evidence. Approved model/speech configuration, verified private routing, bounded provider smoke, actual Android/iOS microphone acceptance and rollback remain deployment gates. Use the [adult release checklist](docs/adult-pilot/VOICE_RELEASE_AND_UAT.md); fixture CI is not that evidence.

original grant; preserved adult revision audience; and active duplicate-report

An active cached technical receipt cannot retain pending evidence after
revocation; terminal receipts remain idempotent acknowledgments without a new
charge or publication. The staged patch whitespace check also passed.

**Actions follow-up, 2026-11-19 05:39:58 UTC:** the fresh merged browser journey
completed the legacy sequence of five edits, Keep/Undo/Fork/Reload and
cancellation, then failed its first v2 Marble Playground creation. The three
attempts reported `RUNTIME_FAILED`, a 30-second `FIXTURE_TIMEOUT`, and
`RUNTIME_FAILED`, exhausting the fixture's repair path. No page/API errors were
observed. That failed observation is retained locally as
`test-results/assignment-002-desktop-failure-merged-v2.json` and its PNG; it is
not relabeled as a pass.

The unchanged isolated v2 fixture subsequently passed in **9,431 ms** with its
technical/device flags true. The complete Actions run then passed serially as
recorded above, with **no source, test, timeout threshold or frozen-runtime
change**. Resource contention is a possible explanation for the earlier run,
not an established cause. The successful rerun establishes the named merged
fixture journeys; hosted final-head CI, real providers and actual devices remain
separate evidence.

**Remaining before generation activation:** final coordinated worker/source/image
pins; appropriate approved multimodal input/spending limits and optional image
approval; live-provider holdouts across visual styles; reviewer calibration against
human judgments; actual-device performance; and evidence-retention verification.
Removing raw screenshots from active job state is not physical erasure from the
existing content-addressed object store. Child-uploaded art and an art-library UI
remain a separate future task.

`7e7fb257e2554b5d106e403e8f491df55c260898`. It does not relabel the earlier

the new interface.

The updated [feature contract](docs/brand/GEM_FACTORY.md) specifies clickable
physical Order/Return buckets, one candidate in flight, repeating gem patterns
with an exact next answer, actions on the illustrated cutter and polisher, and
three wrapping papers that remain visible on shipped gifts. The extra console,
right-hand instructions, stage names and separate Drop/recovery buttons are
removed. Host learning, profile ownership and the existing progress schema are
preserved. This native game revision does not alter generation/review policy or
activate provider services.

| Focused source checks | Final TypeScript and Vite production build passed. The pattern/simulation suites passed 6/6 checks. The updated browser contract passes JavaScript syntax and patch checks; it has not been executed as a full browser suite for this revision. |
| Interactive desktop Site play | CUA verified one complete gift: incorrectly sending Sapphire to Order gently offered the same candidate again; Return advanced to Ruby; Order with Ruby unlocked the actual cutter. Holding and Enter advanced polishing. All three paper choices changed the gift, and the shipped striped gift remained in the completed-gift display at 1/3 with coins awarded. |
| Trusted learning handoff | The host's timed Math prompts paused play and returned to the game during that interactive session. This is integration evidence, not a learning-gain or child-usability claim. |
| Responsive interactive play | A 390 px browser frame completed sorting, cutter and keyboard polishing, selected star paper and shipped its gift; 66 × 73 px paper controls and the gift stayed within the viewport. The camera brought each active station into view. An 820 px frame was visually checked after shipping, including automatic focus-loss pause and normal resume. These are browser frames, not actual mobile hardware. |
| Complete-day and persistence coverage | The updated CI contract retains touch cancellation, complete-day learning, next-day progression and profile/persistence checks. This revision did not re-execute that full browser contract; an assertion in a test file is not a passing result. Earlier successful runs retain their original scope. |
| Publication and exact revision | The actual new captures are linked from the feature contract. PR58 records the final public source/tree and the separate Site publication receipt once issued; the earlier successful Site receipt above remains historical. This source entry alone does not assert a new publication. |

TypeScript and production build passed. The live local preview decoded the exact
1254 × 1254 master; an actual screenshot records its visual placement beside
the hint. This scoped visual check does not replace the hosted gameplay suite.

The current-head check inspection also found one concrete interaction failure
in both the dedicated Gem Factory and nested Studio CI journeys at `23c8190`:
the gift button bobbed continuously, so an ordinary click could not wait for a
stable target. The fix moves that animation to the inner gift artwork while
keeping the actual button stationary. It does not force input or disable motion
to make the test pass. The hosted result for the follow-up commit must establish
whether that journey now passes; earlier 6-test and 351-test receipts retain
their own source scopes. PR58 records current-head checks and the exact Site
publication/merge receipt as each completes.

## How to record the next state

For each changed claim, record **what**, **which repository/commit/image/service**, **environment and audience**, **observation time**, **source link**, **observed result**, and **remaining uncertainty**. For a deployment include the prior working revision/rollback reference. For a test include whether it used fixtures, synthetic real-provider inputs, actual devices, or observed people. Keep private operational/consent evidence in its approved private location and link a safe reference; never copy secrets or child content here.

Use separate terms: **planned**, **implemented on a branch**, **merged**, **deployed to a named environment**, **activated for a named scope**, and **verified for a named criterion**. A green check is evidence only for the check it ran. See [PR template](.github/PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE.md) and [maintenance](docs/context/MAINTENANCE.md).
